Whether a certificate copy ever reached `far-n1` after 07:32:01 was unobservable in that tree: a certificate about the previous hand arriving during the next one was dropped without a line. **Not `S1-BQ`.** That row's client had a copy and did not replay it; this client never had one before its hand ended, so no ordering inside `on_timeout_cert` can help it. The certificate's roster half is carried only by `TIMEOUT_CERT` copies, eight of them, over a carrier that delivered a `HAND_ABORT` from the far box in nine seconds and none of the eight copies in eighteen — `S1-BN`'s open cause, seen from the roster's side. (The far seats' `HAND_INIT(8)` copies from the local seats arrived at 07:32:07–09, the tick-8 re-send, and Tox delivers in order per connection, so the certificate copies sent ten seconds earlier on the same connections most probably arrived just before them — into hand #8's hold, dropped without a line. *Twenty-odd seconds late*, not fifty-one, and the first instrument below is what will say.) **What could close it, for the owner's decision, because each touches §4.10 or the wire:** (1) an unattributed abort is held — `NotYet`, the existing disposition — while a certificate vote this client has joined is open and `long_past_stage` is false, which narrows the race to twice the budget and would not have saved this run (the copies were 51 s late); (2) `HAND_ABORT` carries the emitter's banked certificates, which are self-proving and verified by the receiver like any copy — a wire change, and the emitter here held none; (3) the strongest: a client that opens hand k+1 and hears no `HAND_INIT` within the stage budget treats that as *a genesis nobody holds*, re-derives R(k+1) from the certificates that arrived after its hand ended — they are still self-proving evidence about hand k — and re-opens; that is a late roster repair at the genesis, and it is where §4.9's readmission logic already looks. **The owner picked 3 and 1 (2026-09-05).** Five readers, three designers and two judges went over the two before a line was written; what they settled, and what they left to the owner: (a) the nine never certify the odd seat out — at a crypto stage the vote and the abort fire in the same stall tick at twice the budget (`S1-BT`), so hand k+1 dies at 60 s on the eight and the odd seat stays in R every hand after; the table therefore converges the moment the odd client re-derives `GENESIS(k+1)`, at k+1 if its `HAND_INIT` lands inside the eight's window and at k+2 otherwise, because `ABORT_TERMINAL` is a function of the genesis alone; (b) a second signed `HAND_INIT` from one seat at (k+1, sequence 0) with a different parent is the equivocation §5.2.3 forbids of an honest peer — the parent is excluded from the slot key on purpose — undetected today only because every receiver turns the foreign parent into `NotYet` before the stage hears it; so the repair is built **without** it: a client that already signed `HAND_INIT(k+1)` re-opens the corrected hand as a follower that says nothing at stage 0 and rejoins at k+2, and a client that holds two seats' foreign `HAND_INIT`s *before* it opens does not sign at all until the parent is settled; whether the k+1 road is worth a knowing exception (a D-027 in D-024's shape, with the argument that the first body's parent is a value no seat accepted any chain-(k+1) event under) is the owner's next ruling, not taken here; (c) a certificate banks late only after an **abort** terminal — a settled hand's terminal is the `HAND_COMPLETE` stage hash and a late certificate there is D-024 point 4's fork; (d) option 1 is a no-op at a betting stage, where `past_deadline` is already false and the gate is already twice the budget — it closes the crypto-stage window between one and two budgets, and it is not the fix for this run. **Landing order, one measured run each (`S1-BD`):** first the instruments — the foreign-genesis line, the late-certificate line, the held-queue dedup, the re-send counter (`S1-BU`); then the repair; then option 1. **Landing 1, `f6d60fe`, run `split111249-10` (5+5, 900 s):** 12 hands, one genesis per hand on all ten, four certificates, neither new line fired — no odd seat, nothing to repair, no regression. **Landing 2, `0d97db6` (option 3, D-027), run `split113352-10`:** 15 hands, one genesis per hand on all ten, one certificate, no repair line fired. Three refuters were then run on the *implementation* — the panel had judged the design — and all three found the same defect: the next-hand arm rested on the invariant that its timer fires only about a hand that is over, and a re-open during the boundary wait broke it — `next_hand()` is `None` for a running hand, so the arm would have moved the re-opened hand into `previous` and left the seat with **no hand for the rest of the table**, exactly on the road D-027 point 5 advertises; a muted hand re-opened on a second certificate would have spoken (`spoke()` is false for a muted hand — one seat signing one hand twice); and a quiet hand whose stage 0 completed between two stall ticks never spoke. Fixed in `d388217` before any run relied on them: the arm never succeeds a hand that is not over and a re-open clears the timers, muted stays muted, the sequence gate on speaking is gone — with the refuters' lesser findings: a `NotYet` copy on the retained hand is held there and re-judged (two certificates in either order), the boundary tie counts this seat on its side so a symmetric split deals on as the box says, the buffer and the detector count roster seats of the hand only so two certified-out seats cannot make every honest seat open quietly, a failed re-open keeps its held queue, a repair is kept while frozen and a refused one is said. Two pure functions carry the two decisions with tests. What the refuters could not attack and the register records: the node side has no unit test of `reopen_hand`, the wait or the retained-hand path — the mechanism is proven at hand level (five seats, the whole shape, one genesis at k+2, no second signature) and the node's plumbing by reading and by the runs; an induced-loss run (a harness that drops `TIMEOUT_CERT` copies to one far seat) is the acceptance test still owed. **Landing 3, `2440fb2` (option 1):** the uncertified abort gate admits on the whole-hand budget, on twice the stage budget, and on one stage budget only while no certificate this client is party to is one copy away; a hold is said once per stage; the hold test fails with the old gate, and a second test pins the admission at twice the budget with no certificate. It changes cryptographic stages only and is not the fix for the measured run. **The final tree, run `split115952-10` (5+5, 900 s):** 9 hands, one genesis per hand on every client that opened it, no certificate, no repair line — and the carrier's worst hour of the day: *Send array stuck* on `far-n0` ten times and `far-n1` five, on three local seats seven between them, spread from 182 s to 844 s rather than one blip, `far-n0` giving up hands #3, #4 and #5 on its own budget (#5 after 192 s). Nothing in the three landings touches the carrier, the clean runs before and after them had none of it, and it is `S1-BN`'s open shape; it is recorded here with its numbers and not attributed. **The acceptance test, `ca134c4`:** a fault-harness knob — `P2P_POKER_DELAY_CERTS_MS`, `-DelayCerts <ms> -DelayCertsSeat <far index>` — parks every `TIMEOUT_CERT` one far seat receives for a set time before it is judged, which induces the measured shape (the copies twenty-odd seconds late at one seat) on purpose, so the repair is exercised live instead of waited for. **Attempt 1, `split121953-10` (5+5, 900 s, copies parked 25 s on `far-n1`):** 11 hands, one genesis per hand on all ten — and nothing to park, because **no certificate formed in the whole run**: the best tally anywhere was 5/9, every hand died at twice the budget, and the carrier dropped four far seats at once between 171 s and 197 s (*Send array stuck* 12, 8, 7, 6 and 2 on the five far seats, 1 to 3 on the local ones) — the second carrier-bad run in a row, `S1-BN`'s shape. What the run did show is option 1 at work: 32 *abort from seat X held: a vote this client joined about seat(s) … is open at stage N, M s to twice the budget* lines, one per stage per seat that had voted, none past the bound. Two things follow for the knob. A seat that holds every vote **seals its own copy and banks it**, so the odd seat of the measured run is one that missed the votes too — the knob now parks `TIMEOUT_VOTE` and `TIMEOUT_CERT` alike. And a certificate has to form for there to be anything to bank late, which a calm carrier and one forced outage give: the harness can now take one *local* seat's link down mid-run (`-LinkDownAt <s> -LinkDownFor <s> -LinkDownSeat <i>`, the tree's own `P2P_POKER_LINK_DOWN_AT/FOR`), so an 8+1 run — the carrier shape that has had no timeout all day — certifies that seat while the far seat, parked 50 s, ends the hand at twice the budget with nothing banked, opens the next one quietly on the eight's buffered `HAND_INIT`s, and is repaired when the copies land. **Attempt 2, `split124308-9` (8+1, 600 s; votes and copies parked 50 s on `far-n0`, `n3` dark 150–270 s): the repair, live, first time.** `n3` went dark inside hand #6; at 195 s `far-n0` voted on it (*1/8*) and then heard nothing (parked); the eight sealed at 196.8 s, ended hand #6 and opened #7 at `ef487142` without seat 3 at 225.7 s; `far-n0` ended #6 at twice the budget with nothing banked and opened #7 at `62dfdd4c` with seat 3 still in — the fork, induced. At 225.1 s the detector spoke (*seat(s) [0, 1] opened it at genesis ef487142; this client opened it at 62dfdd4c*), at 227 s with seven seats. At 247.2 s the parked copies were released: *hand #6 (over): cert: about seat 3 banked after the terminal; the roster of hand #7 is re-derived* and, the same second, *hand #7 re-opens at genesis ef487142 with seats [0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8] (was 62dfdd4c with seats [0 … 8]); 7 held event(s) carried over; muted: this seat signed the hand once already* — the table's genesis from the certificate alone, the seven held copies counted at once, no second signature. Then the designed cost of the muted road: the seven waited 60 s for a `HAND_INIT` a muted seat never sends, certified seat 5 out at 257.7 s and played on — 41 hands in 600 s, one genesis per hand on all seven. `far-n0`'s own certificate was parked too (the knob parked everything for the whole run) and landed at 307.3 s, the second its hand #7 ended — *cert: about seat 5 banked after the terminal; the roster of hand #8 is re-derived* — and at 308.1 s the adrift latch closed the story: *the table is at hand 9 and this client reached only 7*, a table of seven playing five-second hands being two hands on in fifty seconds. One line, no hand dealt alone, which is D-027 point 5 verbatim. Two things were wrong and are fixed with this run: the detector's line repeated every two seconds (the once-per-hand gate re-armed each time the node took the note), and the knob needs a bound — `-DelayCertsUntil <s>` parks only frames arriving before that second of the seat's run — so the certificate about the muted seat itself can land in time for it to follow at k+2 as a bystander and be readmitted, which is attempt 3. **Attempt 3, `split125944-9` (8+1, 600 s; parked 40 s for frames before 250 s on `far-n0`, `n3` dark 150–270 s): the whole road, end to end.** `n3` went dark in hand #8; `far-n0` voted at 189 s and heard nothing; the eight sealed at 191.4 s; `far-n0` ended #8 at twice the budget with nothing banked and opened #9 at `94e8bf84` with seat 3 still in — the fork. 221.3 s: the detector, **once** (*seat(s) [6, 8] opened it at genesis 406eebdb*). 231.3 s: the parked copies land — *hand #8 (over): cert: about seat 3 banked after the terminal; the roster of hand #9 is re-derived* and *hand #9 re-opens at genesis 406eebdb with seats [0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8] (was 94e8bf84 …); 7 held event(s) carried over; muted*. 252.9 s: the seven, having waited 60 s for a `HAND_INIT` a muted seat never sends, certified seat 7 (`far-n0`) out of #9 — the designed cost — and this time the certificate about itself was not parked: 252.4 s on `far-n0`, *the table changes: required [0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8] -> [0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 8] … certified [7]*, and **hand #10 opened at `5c94fbc6` on all eight** — `far-n0` back on the table's genesis at k+2, following as a bystander, with §4.9 already saying *seat(s) [7] are heard again at this hand, and dealt in at the next* at hands #11, #13 and #15 on every client (it was never re-dealt, and the parenthesis this row first carried - *the grace after a certificate* - was wrong: `next_hand` filters `self.open.required` in both branches, so R(k+1) is a subset of R(k) and a seat outside R cannot be readmitted by anything; the two-hand allowance is decremented only for seats the same call removes from `required`, so a seat still in R always holds the full allowance and that gate can never be the deciding one. The seat stays out because the roster is monotone - `S1-O`, `S1-BM` and `S1-BV` - and not because an allowance ran down). Hands #10 to #43 at one genesis on the seven. **`far-n0` is not among them past #16:** it followed as a bystander to hand #16, lost that hand's `DECK_INIT` stage to the five-second pause it was still inside, and sat in it for the remaining 283 seconds of the run with no way out - `S1-BW`, found by reading this run again rather than by the line that closed it. **That is D-027 as written, seen whole: fork, detect once, bank late, re-derive, re-open muted, lose k+1, rejoin at k+2, be heard again.** The row is closed; what stays open is named above — the carrier that made the copies late in the first place (`S1-BN`), the table's inability to certify a mid-delivery seat at a cryptographic stage (`S1-BT`), and the k+1 road behind a §5.2.3 exception, which is the owner's. Also worth a word: the abort that ended the hand came from the seat the table had certified out nine seconds earlier, and §4.10's gate admits it on the receiver's own deadline alone, with no regard to the emitter's standing. | FIXED: `src/table/hand.rs` (`open_with`, `bank`, `on_timeout_cert`, `on_hand_abort`, the accessors, seven tests), `src/net/run.rs` (the retained hand, `reopen_hand`, `begin_hand_with`, `quiet_if_contested`, the boundary wait, three tests), `docs/PROTOCOL.md` (§3.1, §4.0 step 10b, §4.4, §4.9), D-024 point 6, D-027 |
| S1-BT | **OPEN: at a cryptographic stage the vote on a silent seat and this client's own abort fire in the same stall tick, at twice the budget — so a seat the carrier counts as mid-delivery can never be certified there, and a table that lost one seat's `HAND_INIT` loses every hand at 60 s and keeps the seat in R.** Read by the `S1-BS` panel and confirmed in `split092359-10`: the eight's hand #8 waited for seat 3 at stage 0; `vote_on_timeouts` holds the vote while the mid-delivery bit is set until `long_past_stage` (twice `crypto_step_timeout_ms`, 60 s), and `may_abandon` waits for the same instant when a certificate is possible; the stall tick runs the vote first and the abort next, so every voter casts its vote at its own 60 s and aborts in the same tick, the vote is `1/7` on every node (`n0` at 07:32:52 and again on hands #9 to #14), no certificate forms, and `took_part` keeps seat 3 in R for every hand after. Which side moves - the abort waits one tick after a vote cast at twice the budget, or the lever is not applied at `HAND_INIT` - is a D-026 question (an honest seat that is merely slow must not lose its place for it) and was the owner's. **FIXED: the owner took design A on 2026-09-05.** The vote round gets **one stage budget of air after this client's own vote**, on both sides of the rule, at cryptographic stages only, with a ceiling of three budgets. Both sides move together and that is the point: the emitter's gate is `may_abandon` and the receiver's is the cause-1 admission, and if only one waited the first seat to reach its own bound would still end the hand everywhere. The receiver's gate also stops asking *has this client voted* and starts asking *is this client needed*: `party_to_vote` replaces `vote_joined`, because a seat whose own timer has not fired yet is still one of the voters the round needs, and under the old test that seat admitted a peer's abort at one budget and killed the round for the seats that had voted. **The bound.** At a cryptographic stage with a certificate possible, this client's own abort now fires at max(2x, own vote + 1x) and never later than 3x, where x is **the stage's own deadline** and not a constant - three budgets of a 30-second cryptographic stage is 90 s, three of a settling stage is three of whatever that stage budgets. Where no certificate is possible nothing moves: the emitter still gives the hand up at **one** budget, which is what `may_abandon` has always done once `past_deadline` passes and `certificate_possible` is false, and the receiver still admits a peer's abort at two, which is the bound `a_held_abort_is_admitted_at_twice_the_budget_without_a_certificate` pins. Betting stages and the whole-hand budget are untouched. **What it costs an honest table:** nothing on a hand with no stall, nothing on a dead seat whose mid-delivery bit is clear (the certificate completes around one budget, well before two), 62 to 72 s instead of a hand lost at 60 s when the bit is set - and the seat leaves R(k+2) instead of every later hand dying the same way - and 90 s instead of 60 s in the worst case, a round that is held and cannot complete. **D-026, stated exactly, because the first draft of this row was too comfortable.** No new way to end a hand is created, and at a cryptographic stage the abort refunds the start-of-hand stacks whichever way it fires, so no chip moves that did not move before. The honest slow seat keeps the full two-budget reprieve **from the abort**. What it does not keep is the reprieve from *removal*, and that is a real change: `S1-BK`'s lever suppresses a vote about a seat the carrier is still delivering only until twice the budget, *whatever the carrier says*, and until now that bound was inert because the abort killed every round before it could gather. Making the round complete makes the bound live, so a seat whose traffic is genuinely still in flight at two budgets now leaves `R(k+1)` where before it kept its place by accident. The corpus already chose that bound - the lever's own comment says a seat could otherwise buy silence for ever - and this row does not reopen it. **If the owner wants the subject to have air too**, the shape is to hold the lever one further budget while the bit is still set, under the same three-budget ceiling: the hand still ends at three, and a seat the carrier is demonstrably still delivering is not removed. That is a second D-026 ruling and it is not taken here. **What the refuters found in the first shape, all four fixed before the commit.** (1) The air was measured from the **first** vote of the stage, and `vote_on_timeouts` walks every seat the stage waits on with the lever applied per seat: a stage waiting on two seats with different bits votes about the unheld one a budget early, spends the air on a round that has not started, and votes and aborts in the same tick for the second subject - the collision again, measured in `split092359-10` (`far-n3` at 259.6 s: seat 7 voted with its bit clear, seats 1 and 9 held). It is measured from the last vote now, and the ceiling still bounds it because past twice the budget the lever suppresses nothing, so every owed vote of that stage is cast in the first tick beyond it. **The collision was traced end to end in three places the corpus already held**, which is the strongest evidence in this row: `split094546-10` `n1` votes about seat 0 at 410.6 s with its bit clear and then, at 440.7 s, casts seat 8's vote and gives the hand up in the same millisecond; `n4` of the same run at 408.8 and 438.9 s; `split121953-10` `n2` at 290.4 and 320.4 s. Seventy-seven lines across the runs show one stage holding both bit values at once, so the shape is ordinary rather than a corner. With the air taken from the last vote the held subject's round now ends at the ceiling, which is where the strictest reading says it has to be. (2) The air predicate asked `party_to_vote` while its caller `may_abandon` asks `certificate_possible`, and the two disagree for a client whose own seat was certified out mid-hand: it would have aborted at two budgets while every voter held to three, ending hand k bare while the rest ended it by certificate - two rosters from one chain. The predicate asks `certificate_possible` now; `party_to_vote` stays at the receive gate's third clause, where the question really is whether this client is needed. (3) `may_abandon` had no crypto-stage escape, so a betting stage that had spent the **whole-hand** budget had its backstop deferred by a vote round - `past_deadline` returns true on `hand_deadline_ms` before it looks at the stage. It mirrors the receive gate now. (4) A test asserted `is_err()` where the claim was *held*: only `NotYet` is parked for the replay, every other error is a reject and the abort is gone. **Three tests pin it and all three fail against the old bounds** (checked by simulating them, not by argument): a lever-held round gets its air and certifies at 70 s instead of being aborted at 62 s; a round that cannot complete still ends the hand at exactly three budgets; a voter that owes a vote it has not cast holds the abort; and a second round opened at the same stage gets its own air rather than the first round's, which is defect (1) above and is the one the three-seat fixture could not see. **The run came: `split142458-9`, 8+1, 900 s, `-Think 2000`, one local seat's link down for 70 s from 300 s - and it passes on every clause.** Two departures from the prescription above, both deliberate: **8+1 rather than 5+5**, because the far box has four cores and five seats there would measure starvation; and `-Think 2000`, because until today every run in the corpus played at zero think time, where hands are 14.4 s and `ADRIFT_MARGIN` = 2 is a 29-second wall - so a seventy-second outage was five hands of drift and the seat latched out before this row's own mechanism could be seen (`S1-BW`, `S1-CC`). At 2 000 ms a hand is about 76 s and the outage costs well under one. **What the run shows, in the seat's own words.** Two vote rounds about seat 4 inside hand #3, and **both close in a certificate rather than an abort**: at 359.5-361.3 s *"seat 4 @4a630ea7: 1/8 ... 8/8 agree"*, *"my clock has run out on seat 4 - I am at stage 43, 4 last heard at stage 41"*, then **"the table has certified seat 4's timeout, unanimously among [0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8]"** and *"the table acted for seat 4: Check"* - the hand carries on. The second round runs at 406.1-408.0 s at stage 51 against last-heard 43, certifies unanimously again, and only then does hand #3 end on the hand deadline. That is the shape this row was built for: the round gets its air, reaches unanimity, and the outcome is a certificate. **One hand lost, not every hand.** Exactly nine aborts in the whole run - hand #3 at each of the nine clients - and hands #4 through #10 run at ~76 s each. **Ten hands, one genesis each, all nine clients opening every one**, with the roster narrowing **exactly once**: `[0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]` for hands #1-#3 and `[0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8]` for #4-#10. So hand k+2 opens without the seat at one genesis on every client, which is the clause verbatim. **And the certified seat kept following:** `n3` opened hands #4 to #10 in lockstep with the founder, a few seconds ahead of it each time, and **nothing latched anywhere in the run**. | FIXED: `src/table/hand.rs` (`party_to_vote`, `round_has_had_air`, `own_vote_at`, `may_abandon`, the cause-1 gate, three tests) - **fixed and accepted** |
| S1-BU | **FIXED: the power-of-two re-send counter was keyed on the sequence alone, so across hands that all end at sequence 0 it never reset.** The stall tick repeats the last three stages of the hand at ticks 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 … of the *stage*, restarting when `slot().sequence` changes; a run of hands that die at stage 0 — `S1-BT`'s shape — leaves it at 0 across every boundary, so the new hand's terminal and `HAND_INIT` went out at ticks 32, 64 and 128 (64, 128 and 256 s apart): once a hand at best, then never. Every convergence argument in the `S1-BS` designs assumed 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 s of the new hand's stage 0; the judge that checked the cadence found it. The counter is now keyed on the hand as well as the sequence. | FIXED: `src/net/run.rs` (the re-send loop) |
| S1-BV | **The log promises a readmission the roster never performs, and it promised it six times a node about one seat in a single run.** `src/net/run.rs` printed *"seat(s) [7] are heard again at this hand, and dealt in at the next"* at the opening of hands #12 to #17 on `n0`, `n1`, `n2`, `n4`, `n5`, `n6` and `n7` of `split125944-9`, and no seat was ever dealt in: every *hand #N opens* line from #10 to #38 lists the same seven seats, and #39 to #43 shrink only by busts. The readmission set `A` is written by `checkpoint_event`'s `Took::Bystander` arm and read in exactly one place - it widens the **accepted** set of the next hand's stage 0, so a seat in it may sign that stage and nothing more. R(k+1) is derived by filtering R(k), in both branches, so a seat outside R cannot re-enter by being heard from, and `dealt_in` filters `o.required` in turn. The line was false in its second clause every time it fired, and it fires for a seat that has **busted** as well - `n0` promised to deal seat 2 in at 600.6 s with a stack of zero. **Corrected here**, in two sentences rather than one: a seat in `A` that is still in the roster IS dealt into this hand, because `A` is written from the checkpoint stage’s `signed` set while the roster is derived from `certified`, and the old line asserted the opposite for every seat it named. The correction also nearly cost something: the first draft dropped this client’s own seat from `readmitted` so that a node would stop announcing its own return, and `open_with` builds the accepted set of stage 0 from that field, so a certified-out bystander would have lost the one signature §4.9 gives it. The set is whole and the filter is on the sentence. So that a reader counting readmissions from a grep does not count six where there were none; the door itself is `S1-BM`'s return certificate and is the owner's. **And the specification is on the losing side of this, in two normative places.** PROTOCOL.md 4.4 says *"a seat readmitted through `A` is dealt in at hand `k+1` rather than at hand `k` - one hand of latency, which 4.9 states as the cost"*, and 4.9 says the seat *"becomes a required emitter, and dealable, at hand `m+2`, one hand later than the deleted rule promised"*. Neither happens: both branches of `next_hand` filter `self.open.required`. D-024 made the roster monotone after those sentences were written and a numbered decision beats a specification, so this is stale text rather than an open question - but which sentence carries the correction is the same decision as the door itself, and it is left with `S1-BM`. Two further readings, both checked and neither acted on: PROTOCOL.md 4.9 promises a `HAND_INIT(m+1)` copy from a seat in `A` is *"accepted, retained and counted into P(m+1)"* until `TERMINAL(m+1)`, and the code has that retention band for the checkpoint's `STATE_ACK` and not for `HAND_INIT`, which is dropped once stage 0 has closed - 60 to 90 ms after it opened, a window no far seat can make; and a node writes `A` about **itself**, so the far seat readmitted itself every other hand. | FIXED: `src/net/run.rs` (the readmission line), `docs/PROTOCOL.md` 4.9 against `src/table/hand.rs` (`on_event`'s past-stage drop) - line fixed, the door is `S1-BM`'s |
| S1-BW | **OPEN, liveness: a seat that follows a hand it is not dealt into can lose the stage it needs and has no way out. Measured at 283 seconds in one hand, ended only by the harness.** `split125944-9`, `far-n0` (seat 7), certified out at 252.9 s and following as a bystander. It opened hand #16 at 318.3 s, said it was waiting for the seven, and never logged another line of that hand: the same status every 30 s from 331.2 s to 571.2 s, *"stage 1 open 271 s"* at 590.2 s, and then the harness. **What it lost.** `n0` sent `DECK_INIT(16)` at 317.6 s of its own clock, about two seconds before `far-n0` opened #16 - measured against a clock offset taken five times from the same pair of logs - while `far-n0` was still inside hand #15's five-second pause. An event of hand k+1 that arrives then is *another hand*: worth relaying, not worth holding, and dropped without a line. Only two kinds survive that window - a `TIMEOUT_CERT` about hand k-1 and a `HAND_INIT` of hand k+1, `S1-BS`'s buffer - so the seven `HAND_INIT(16)` copies were replayed at open, stage 0 closed at once, and stage 1 opened with every `DECK_INIT` already gone. The senders' re-send repeats only the last three stages and had passed that one within 0.3 s. **Why there is no terminus.** `may_abandon` returns false for a non-member, by design and correctly: 4.10 gives a seat outside P(k) no legal terminal to emit. The comment beside it says *"if neither comes it stalls, which is what the adrift latch is for"*, and that is false for a running hand - the latch is set by the later hands' `HAND_INIT` copies as designed, but its only acting reader is the next-hand timer arm, which is reached only once the running hand is over. The latch fired and nothing read it. **For a dealt-in seat the same loss heals itself** - the table cannot leave stage 1 without that seat's `DECK_INIT` and keeps repeating it - which is why this shape had not been seen before: a bystander is waited for by nobody. **FIXED with the first of three, and the other two are recorded as not taken.** The boundary now keeps **every** verified event of hand k+1, not only its `HAND_INIT`, in a second buffer capped at thirty-two, drained into the hand's own queue at open behind the `HAND_INIT`s and re-judged by the same replay that already runs there. A pure function decides what an event of a hand this client has not opened is worth, with a test. **Stage traffic only, and one seat cannot fill it** - both corrections came from the refuters. A terminal, a vote or a certificate of the next hand is **not** carried into it: replayed out of that queue it would end or narrow a hand the hand was opened without, and a hand opened quiet, which is withholding its signature on purpose, could emit a terminal from it. Each of the four has its own road already. And the sibling buffer needs no per-seat bound because a seat has one HAND_INIT per hand, while a seat may sign as much stage traffic as it likes - without a bound one seated peer fills all thirty-two slots and the bystander loses the stage it was waiting for, which is this fault re-created by its own fix. Six per seat. **The two buffers stay apart on purpose:** `quiet_if_contested` reads the `HAND_INIT` buffer to decide which genesis this client opens at, and nothing else may reach that decision. The cap is thirty-two because the hand's own queue holds sixty-four and evicts the oldest, so sixteen `HAND_INIT`s plus thirty-two of these leaves a third of it for what arrives after the hand opens; the measured case needed seven. **Not taken, and why:** a follower's local terminus (a non-member past twice the budget ends the hand without emitting) is a change to what 4.10 permits and wants its own ruling; making the adrift latch act mid-hand is what `may_abandon`'s comment already claims and would end a running hand on two peers' word, which is a wider change than the measured fault needs. Both stay available if a run shows the buffer is not enough. **The test is a classification test and not a regression pin, and that is worth saying:** the decision it checks lives in a pure function, but the behaviour it changes lives in the node loop, where the old code simply dropped the frame - so no unit test can fail against the tree as it was. What is owed is therefore a run, and it is the real acceptance: a bystander opens a hand later than the table dealt it and completes it. **Two attempts, neither of which reproduced the shape, and the reason is worth more than the attempts.** `split214929-9` used the new deafness knob: the deaf seat fell seven hands behind and the adrift latch removed it at 408.4 s, so it never followed anything. `split220636-9` used `split125944-9`'s own recipe - certificates parked at the far seat, one local seat's link down for 120 s - and the local seat gave two hands up on its own deadline and latched out at 299.4 s, while the far seat joined so late that it opened hand #1 when the table was at hand 14 and latched out immediately. Both times the table itself was healthy: 34 hands, one genesis each except the hand the departing seat forked and then left. **What that says is that the bystander state is much narrower than this row assumed.** A seat has to be certified out **and** stay inside `ADRIFT_MARGIN`, two hands, of the table it is following - and every fault instrument in the tree pushes it further than that, because the same silence that gets a seat certified also costs it hands. `split125944-9` reached it by a coincidence this row can now name: the seat's **own** certificates were parked, so it forked, repaired, re-opened muted, and was certified out at k+1 while still level with the table. Reproducing that on demand needs a knob that certifies a seat without making it fall behind, and none existed. **`S1-CC` built it**: `P2P_POKER_MUTE_AT` / `_FOR`, a seat that hears everything and is heard by nobody, so it misses its decision deadline, is certified out at `k`, and is still level with the table at `k+1`. The same row also found that the outage knob one of these two attempts used was never silent, which is a second reason `split220636-9` did not test what it was set up to test. **The run, `split124559-9`, 8+1, 600 s, mute on one local seat, and it is half an answer.** Seat 8 was certified out at **320.6 s** - *"the table changes: required [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8] -> [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7] ... certified [8] ... by_certificate=true"* - and then followed the table for **twenty-one more hands**, opening each one at the table's own pace: #14 at 320.6 s where the founder opened it at 325.4 s, #20 at 398.1 s against 402.5 s. **Certified out and level**, which is the state two earlier attempts could not reach and which no other knob in the tree produces. The table stayed healthy through it: **35 hands, one genesis each, all nine seats on every one** - the bystander included, which is `D-024`'s monotone roster holding for twenty-one hands at a seat that is in none of them. **And the buffer did not fire, for a reason worth more than the run:** a bystander that is level opens the next hand **first**. It owes the table nothing, so nothing delays it, and no frame of `k+1` can arrive before it opens - the buffer had nothing to keep. So the shape needs a second thing on the same seat: it must learn of its own removal **late**, which is what `split125944-9` had by coincidence when its own certificates were parked. `-DelayCertsHere` puts that knob on a local seat (it was wired for far seats only), and the two together are the recipe: the mute certifies it out without letting it fall behind, the delay makes it re-derive and re-open `k+1` after the table has dealt it. **One more thing this run bought:** the carry is now said out loud - *"hand #N: X init(s) and Y early frame(s) had arrived before it opened here"* - because until this run a log in which the fix worked and a log in which nothing arrived early were the same log. **The second run, `split130616-9`, took the mute and the cert delay together and produced a third outcome, and its lesson is the sharpest thing this row has.** The seat was certified out at **216.0 s**, inside the mute - and at **239.3 s** it said *"this client is out: the table is at hand 8 and this client reached only 6, so it has been dealing a hand nobody else has"*. **A muted seat cannot finish the hand it is muted in**: its own contribution never leaves, so it sits in that one hand for the whole window while the table deals on. Measured in that run: mean hand period **14.4 s**, `ADRIFT_MARGIN` = 2, so the latch tolerates about **29 s** of that - and certifying the muted seat took **39 s** of silence. **The two windows are disjoint at this table's rate.** A mute long enough to be certified out is long enough to be latched out, and that is the same wall both earlier attempts hit; `split124559-9` cleared it only by accident, because its clock was anchored at the first hand send so the certificate landed after the seat could speak again (`S1-CC`). **So the instrument for this shape is not silence at all** - it is `split125944-9`'s own recipe, the cert delay **alone**: a seat that never stops speaking is never adrift, and parking the certificates it needs makes it fork, repair and re-open `k+1` after the table has dealt it, which is the re-open this row exists for. **And the new line already paid for itself.** Across `split130616-9`'s nine seats it fired **69 times on a healthy table** - and every one of them reads *N init(s) and **0** early frame(s)*. So the older `HAND_INIT` buffer is ordinary weather at every boundary at every seat, while `next_early`, the buffer **this row added**, has still never carried a frame in any run on disk. That is worth knowing before anybody counts the older buffer's traffic as evidence for the newer one. **And the third run turned that absence into a proof, which is worth more than the run it came from.** `split131946-9` took the cert delay alone - `split125944-9`'s own recipe - and produced **zero certificates in 40 hands**: parking the certificates a seat receives does nothing when nothing is making any seat miss a deadline, so the precondition never arose. But its numbers said something better. The spread of open times across seats is a **median 7.0 s** and a seat emits its first deck event **0.2 s** after opening - so on the face of it every late opener should be buried in early frames, and none of them keeps a single one. The reason is structural: **`HAND_INIT` is a collective stage** (`Phase::Init(stage)`, and the hand does not leave it until `waiting_for()` is empty), so **no seat can emit a stage-1 frame of hand `k+1` until every seat in `R(k+1)` has opened it**. A member of `R` therefore cannot be overtaken; the spread is the collective stage waiting, not the table running ahead. It follows that **`next_early` can only ever fill at a client outside `R(k+1)`** - exactly the bystander this row was opened for, and nothing else. The 69 and 66 readings of *0 early frame(s)* on healthy tables are the correct answer, not a miss, and no run of a healthy table can ever be the acceptance. **What that costs the acceptance, stated as a bound.** The bystander must be outside `R(k+1)` **and** still following, and the only road out of `R` is a certificate, which needs silence: every seat in `R` owes something in every hand, so silencing one always stalls the hand it is in and the drift grows with the silence. Certification took **39 s** of it; the adrift latch tolerates `ADRIFT_MARGIN` = 2 hands, **29 s** at this table's 14.4-second hands. So the instrument this needs is not another fault knob but a **slower table** - a delay on each client's own autoplay action, which makes the two-hand wall minutes rather than seconds and leaves the certification time where it is. **And it did not need building: `--autoplay` has taken a millisecond argument since it was written and this harness had never passed one**, so every run in the corpus is a table playing at zero think time - which is also why hands are 14.4 s and the wall is 29. `-Think <ms>` passes it, to every seat, local and far. At 3 000 ms a hand is about a minute, so a fifty-second mute costs the muted seat less than one hand where at full speed it cost five. A run taken with it measures a slow table and its formation and hand counts are **not comparable with the rest of the corpus**; the harness says so in its header. **And the acceptance run came, at the fourth attempt: `split135059-9`, 8+1, 900 s, `-Think 3000`, a fifty-second mute and a twenty-second certificate delay on one local seat. The buffer fired.** Seat 3 was certified out at 398.0 s, learned it late, and at **415.5 s** said *"hand #2: 8 init(s) and 32 early frame(s) had arrived before it opened here"* - then opened hand #2 at **415.8 s**, sixteen seconds after the table did (the founder opened it at 399.5 s), **at the table's own genesis `7566d0a1` and with the table's own roster** `[0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]`. That is the bystander this row was opened for, doing the thing it could not do before: the frames of a hand it was not there for were kept and replayed into it. **And the same run says the fix is necessary and not sufficient, which is worth more than a pass.** The hand did not complete. It opened *waiting for seat 5* and was still waiting **484 seconds later**, at the end of the run, while the table played hands #3, #4, #5 and #6. **And the account this row first gave of why is wrong in three places, corrected here from the same logs.** The buffer did not stop at stage 0: the replay carried this client straight through **stages 0 to 17** and stalled at **18**, which at eight dealt-in seats is `DECK_COMMIT` - sixteen lines from `n3.log:6925` read *"not voting: stage 18 open 31 s ... for Some(DeckCommit) ... seat 5: subject yes"*. It was one `DECK_COMMIT`, about 101 bytes, and **not** a `HAND_INIT`: all eight of those arrived. Nor was seat 5 unheard: *"5 silent 47s"* is a **libp2p ping RTT** (`alive`, written only from `Ping` and `ConnectionEstablished`) while a hand rides Tox and nothing else, and the same line says *"tox self udp, group 8 seen/8 confirmed/8 wanted, tox friends up 8"*. Nor was seat 5 on hand #3 by 450 s - it sat in hand #2 until 501.6 s, holding that frame in its own `said` throughout. **What actually happened is a receiver bug in this row's own buffer**, and it is `S1-CD`: seat 5 sealed the deck **18.4 s before** this client opened hand #2, so the pre-open buffer was that frame's only route in - and the buffer held exactly **32**, which is `NEXT_EARLY_CAP` exactly, while a nine-handed table's run before the first bet is **40 frames**. The buffer was full and refused rather than evicted. **That is the shape of what is still missing, and this row already named it before it had a run:** *"that is what a catch-up request is for, and it does not exist yet"*. The buffer closes the window where the frame arrives and is thrown away; it cannot close the window where the frame never arrives. The two wider changes this row kept in reserve are both bigger buffers, and neither would have saved this hand. | OWED: `src/net/run.rs` (the two next-hand buffers, `keep_for_next_hand`, `begin_hand_with`, one test), `src/table/hand.rs` (`may_abandon`’s comment, corrected) - **fixed and measured: the buffer works and is not enough**; what is owed now is a catch-up request, not a bigger buffer |
| S1-BX | **FIXED, three defects in the `S1-BS` node code, all three found by reading it and none by a run.** (1) **A repair could be dropped at the boundary.** A late certificate banks in the retained hand and leaves a re-opening for the stall tick to apply two seconds later; a peer's `HAND_ABORT` arms the next-hand timer 800 ms out. When the timer won, the arm succeeded the very hand the repair was about, hand k left retention, and the tick's re-open then refused on the hand id - the seat would have dealt hand k+2 at a genesis of its own, on the road D-027 point 5 advertises. The arm now yields to a pending repair and re-arms. (2) **The voter-set shortfall note was said on every replay pass**, which is every two seconds for as long as the copy is held, one line per held copy, and unbounded during the boundary wait. It is said once per subject digest now. (3) **A redelivered certificate was reported as a repair missed**: *"hand #k is no longer held here, so it cannot repair anything"* fired four seconds after the same certificate had banked and re-derived the roster, twice in the day's runs (`split125944-9` banked at 231.3 s and said it at 235.3 s; `split124308-9` 247.2 s and 251.2 s), because the second copy arrived after retention had ended. The line now adds what the node can actually prove - that a certificate about that hand did bank here before retention ended - and stops asserting that nothing was lost, because banking is keyed on the subject digest and one hand can certify two seats. None of the three changes a disposition, and none of them touches the wire. | FIXED: `src/net/run.rs` (the next-hand arm, the late-certificate arm, the stall tick), `src/table/hand.rs` (`on_timeout_cert`) - fixed |
| S1-BY | **OPEN: a seat certified out in the middle of a hand still believes it is a member of that hand, and emits a terminal 4.10 does not allow it.** `may_abandon` refuses for a non-member by testing `self.open.required`, which is the roster **as the hand opened** and does not shrink when a certificate about this seat banks mid-hand; `self.certified` is what grows, and nothing reads it there. Measured in `split092359-10`, `far-n0` (seat 1): at 327.3 s it banks *"a certificate from seat 0 about seat 1"* - about itself - and at 334.0 s, still inside the same stage, it gives the hand up on its own budget and prints the roster change that proves `required` still holds it. It did no harm that day because every other seat aborted at the same instant. The adrift latch is read only in the next-hand timer arm, which a hand that never ends never reaches. So the guard converts an emission into a permanent stall: `S1-BW`'s fault moved onto a different seat. **(2) The premise was false, and the run this row cites says so.** In `split092359-10` the certified seat's abort **was** the terminal the table adopted: `far-n0` gives hand #5 up at 07:29:54.588 and `n0`, `n1`, `n2` and `far-n2` log *"a peer ended the hand on its own deadline"* within 80 to 230 ms, and no other node logs a deadline abort for that hand anywhere in the run. It did not fork either: every node opened hands #6 to #15 at byte-identical genesis, because `abort_terminal` is a function of `GENESIS(k)` alone. The row's own *"it did no harm while every seat abandoned in the same instant"* had it backwards - the others did not abandon, they were freed. **(3) `certified` grows for a kind-1 certificate too**, which removes nobody from the hand, so one auto-fold would have disarmed that seat's only backstop for the rest of it. **(4) The guard sat above the whole-hand budget**, making the one deadline the protocol keeps unconditional conditional, and the test pinned that deliberately. **(5) It asks an R(k+1) question in a P(k) place.** **And the obvious follow-up is closed too:** restricting the guard to kind-2 certificates does not save it. A kind-2 certificate does not narrow `open.required` either, and `bank` writes `certified` on receipt of the **first** voter’s copy, before the stage completes - so a kind-2-only guard still opens an unceilinged window in which the seat can neither abort nor be aborted. | S1-BZ | **Three chained event types are declared on the wire and the state machine answers all three with *wrong type*.** `PLAYER_SIT_OUT` (`0x0803`), `PLAYER_SIT_IN` (`0x0804`) and `PLAYER_LEAVE` (`0x0805`) are in the catalogue, are chained, are boundary events with their own sequence rule, and `BOUNDARY_SEQUENCE_BASE` is referenced by nothing outside its own compile-time assertions. `Hand::on_event`'s type gate lists eighteen types and none of these, so a conforming peer that emits one gets `Failed::Wire(WrongType)`; the node then treats it as a formation message and nothing consumes it.
